------------------------------
**Step 1:** Analyze the spatial contradiction between evidences and testimonies.

Lang's Testimony 3 states that the president's body was hidden "inside that monster costume over there." However, Evidence 5 (Crime Scene Notes) indicates that the body was found "lying face-up on top of the dirt," with no dirt on the front of the body. This suggests the body was placed on top of the dirt, not inside the costume.

**Step 2:** Check if any spatial propositions from the list above apply to this contradiction.

Prop 1 states: "President Wang's body is inside the monster costume." This directly contradicts the spatial description in Evidence 5, which places the body on top of the dirt, not inside the costume.

**Step 3:** If applicable propositions exist, explicitly state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction.

Prop 1 ("President Wang's body is inside the monster costume") contradicts Evidence 5, which describes the body as lying on top of the dirt with no dirt on the front, implying it was not inside the costume where dirt would have been present.

**Step 4:** If none of the listed propositions apply, explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction.

Not applicable, as Prop 1 directly applies.

**The answer JSON should strictly be the last line, with nothing before or after it.**

LLM OUTPUT (RAW RESPONSE):
------------------------------
**Step 1:** Analyze the spatial contradiction between evidences and testimonies

Looking at White's testimonies, he claims:
- Testimony 1: "A light stand was lying on the floor when I looked."
- Testimony 2: "Then, the next moment, I saw Miss Mia run to the left!"
- Testimony 3: "The killer, you, attacked her... but she dodged."

However, Evidence 8 (Floor Plans) shows that "The stand is blocked from view looking from the office."

**Step 2:** Check if any spatial propositions apply to this contradiction

Prop 10 states: "If the stand is blocked from view looking from the office then White cannot see the stand from his office."

**Step 3:** Apply the relevant proposition

According to Prop 10, since the floor plans (Evidence 8) explicitly state that the stand is blocked from view looking from White's office, White cannot see the stand from his office. This directly contradicts White's testimony (Testimony 1) where he claims "A light stand was lying on the floor when I looked."

**Step 4:** The contradiction is clearly revealed by Prop 10

The spatial proposition confirms that White's claim to have seen the light stand on the floor is impossible given the floor plans showing the stand is blocked from his view.

{"evidence": 8, "testimony": 1}

------------------------------
**Step 1:** Analyze the spatial contradiction between evidences and testimonies

Looking at Atmey's testimonies, we have two key spatial claims:
- Testimony 5: "I put my back to the wall to fight, but the thief's blow landed upon my third eye."
- Testimony 2: "the thief grabbed a weapon from the side and... rendered me senseless."

From Evidence 6 (Shichishito), we know: "DeMasque knocked Atmey out during the crime w/ a blow to the back of the head."

**Step 2:** Check if any spatial propositions apply to this contradiction

The contradiction involves:
1. Where the blow came from (back vs. front/third eye)
2. Atmey's position relative to the wall

**Step 3:** Apply relevant spatial propositions

- Prop 8: "If Atmey's back is to the wall then the wall is behind Atmey"
- Prop 9: "If Mask☆DeMasque strikes Atmey's third eye then Mask☆DeMasque is in front of Atmey"
- Prop 10: "If Atmey is rendered senseless by a blow to the back of the head then the blow comes from behind Atmey"

**Step 4:** Explain the contradiction using spatial reasoning

Atmey claims in Testimony 5 that he had his back to the wall (Prop 8: wall is behind him) and was struck on his third eye (Prop 9: Mask☆DeMasque must be in front of him). However, Evidence 6 states he was knocked out by a blow to the back of the head (Prop 10: the blow must come from behind him). 

This creates an impossible spatial situation: if Atmey's back is to the wall, no one can be behind him to deliver a blow to the back of his head. The evidence shows the knockout blow came from behind, but Atmey's testimony places the wall behind him and the attacker in front.

These propositions establish that the P.I.C. meeting room is contained within the Committee chamber (Props 1-2), and that any blood in the Committee chamber would necessarily be in the P.I.C. meeting room (Props 6-7, 15).

**Step 3:** If applicable propositions exist, explicitly state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

The contradiction arises between:
- Testimony 3: Claims the crime scene is specifically the P.I.C. meeting room
- Evidence 21: Suggests blood was found in the Committee chamber (which would include the P.I.C. meeting room according to Props 6-7)

However, the phrasing in Evidence 21 implies the blood was found in the Committee chamber *separate from* or *in addition to* the P.I.C. meeting room, which contradicts the spatial relationship established in Props 1-2, 6-7, and 15 that the P.I.C. meeting room is entirely contained within the Committee chamber.

**Step 4:** If none of the listed propositions apply, explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

The spatial reasoning shows that if blood was found in the Committee chamber, it would necessarily be in the P.I.C. meeting room (since the meeting room is inside the chamber). Evidence 21's implication that blood was found in the Committee chamber to hide the storeroom suggests this blood was distinct from the blood in the P.I.C. meeting room, which contradicts the spatial containment relationship.

**Step 5:** Provide your answer in JSON format

{"evidence": 21, "testimony": 3}

  "evidence": 21,
  "testimony": 3

**Step 1:** Analyze the spatial contradiction between evidences and testimonies

Looking at the evidence and testimonies, we need to find a spatial contradiction. Testimony 3 states: "The next moment, Wright picked up a bottle and swung it!" This suggests Wright swung the bottle in a horizontal or downward motion to strike Smith.

However, Crime Photo 1 (Evidence 2) shows Smith "slumped in a chair and leaning back" with his hat covering his eyes and forehead. The Deadly Bottle (Evidence 3) has Wright's prints on it, but they are described as "upside-down." Props 3-15 deal with spatial relationships between objects, bodies, and directions, but none directly address the orientation of fingerprints on the bottle.

None of the listed propositions directly address the contradiction between the testimony describing a swinging motion and the evidence showing upside-down fingerprints.

**Step 4:** If none of the listed propositions apply, explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

The spatial contradiction lies in the orientation of the fingerprints. If Wright "picked up a bottle and swung it" as described in Testimony 3, the natural motion would result in right-side-up fingerprints. However, Evidence 3 shows the prints are upside-down, which would require Wright to hold the bottle inverted (bottom-up) while swinging it - an unnatural and unlikely motion for striking someone. This suggests the bottle was placed or positioned rather than swung in the manner described.

2. The testimony about how Wright took the bottle is inaccurate

**Step 4:** If none of the listed propositions apply, explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

While the listed propositions don't directly address "upside-down" prints, the spatial contradiction is clear: Testimony 5 describes Wright taking the bottle in a normal grip to strike Smith, but Evidence 3 shows the prints are upside-down, indicating an unnatural grip that contradicts the described action.

**The answer JSON should strictly be the last line, with nothing before or after it.**

LLM OUTPUT (RAW RESPONSE):
------------------------------
**Step 1:** Analyze the spatial contradiction between evidences and testimonies

Looking at the evidence and testimonies, we need to find a spatial contradiction. The key evidence is Crime Photo 1 (Evidence 2) which shows Smith "slumped in a chair and leaning back." This indicates Smith fell backward. 

From Olga's testimonies, particularly Testimony 3 states "The one who was winning... dah, it was victim!" This suggests Smith was winning the poker game. Testimony 5 states "The moment loss was decided, defendant grabs bottle from table and..." implying Wright grabbed the bottle and struck Smith.

**Step 2:** Check if any spatial propositions apply to this contradiction

The spatial propositions that apply here are:
- Prop 3: If Smith was hit from the front then Smith falls backward
- Prop 4: If Smith was hit from behind then Smith falls forward

**Step 3:** Apply the propositions to reveal the contradiction

Since Crime Photo 1 shows Smith leaning back (falling backward), according to Prop 3, this means Smith was hit from the front. However, if Smith was winning (Testimony 3) and Wright grabbed the bottle from the table (Testimony 5), this would place Wright in front of Smith when striking. This creates a consistent scenario where Wright hits Smith from the front, causing Smith to fall backward.

But there's a deeper contradiction: if Smith was winning (Testimony 3), then Wright would be the one losing and thus would be the one grabbing the bottle in frustration. The spatial arrangement shows Smith fell backward, which is consistent with being hit from the front by Wright. However, the actual contradiction lies in the implication that if Smith was winning, Wright would have been across the table from him, making it impossible for Wright to hit Smith from the front while Smith was sitting in a chair - Wright would need to be behind Smith to hit him from the front while Smith is seated.

**Step 4:** The spatial reasoning reveals that the testimony about Smith winning (Testimony 3) contradicts the spatial evidence in Crime Photo 1 (Evidence 2) showing Smith fell backward, as this would require Wright to be in a position that doesn't align with the poker table setup.

**Step 5:** {"evidence": 2, "testimony": 3}

According to Melissa's testimony (Testimony 4), "The victim turned around and tried to run away, but..." This implies Valerie changed direction from facing left to facing right (running away).

However, Testimony 6 states "She only got about 10 yards before she was stabbed in the back." This creates a spatial contradiction because:

**Step 2:** Check if any spatial propositions apply to this contradiction

Several propositions apply:
- Prop 8: If Valerie turns around and tries to run away then Valerie changes from facing left to facing right
- Prop 9: If Valerie runs 10 yards from the broken end towards the west then Valerie moves away from the broken end
- Prop 5: If Valerie is standing close to the broken end and facing left then Valerie's back is to the broken end
- Prop 1: If Valerie is stabbed in the back then Valerie falls forward
- Prop 2: If Valerie falls forward and the broken end of the bridge is in front of Valerie then Valerie falls off the bridge

**Step 3:** Apply the propositions to reveal the contradiction

According to the propositions:
- Valerie was facing left with her back to the broken end (Prop 5)
- When she turned to run away, she would face right (Prop 8)
- Running 10 yards west would move her away from the broken end (Prop 9)
- However, if she was stabbed in the back while running away (facing right), the knife would come from behind her (east side), but this would mean her back was no longer to the broken end
- If she fell forward from being stabbed, she would fall away from the broken end, not off the bridge (contradicting the scenario where she should fall off)

The key contradiction is that if Valerie successfully ran 10 yards away from the broken end, she would no longer be in danger of falling off the bridge when stabbed, yet the evidence suggests she fell off the bridge.

**Step 4:** The spatial propositions clearly show this contradiction

The propositions demonstrate that if Valerie ran 10 yards west (away from the broken end), she would be safe from falling off the bridge when stabbed. However, the crime scene suggests she fell off the bridge, which would only happen if she was still close to the broken end when stabbed.

LLM OUTPUT (RAW RESPONSE):
------------------------------
**Step 1:** Analyze the spatial contradiction between evidences and testimonies

Looking at L'Belle's testimonies, particularly Testimony 4: "In shock, I FLED down the hallway to my right -- the one with the PHONE at the end." This suggests L'Belle fled toward the phone.

However, examining the Crime Scene Diagram (Evidence 5), we see that at the corridor juncture, the phone is to the north, the foyer is to the south, and the corridor leading to the Fox Chamber is to the east.

**Step 2:** Check if any spatial propositions from the list above apply to this contradiction

Looking at the spatial propositions:
- Prop 4: The phone is to the north of the corridor juncture
- Prop 10: The corridor juncture is between the foyer and the phone

**Step 3:** If applicable propositions exist, explicitly state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

Prop 4 and Prop 10 confirm that the phone is located to the north of the corridor juncture. However, L'Belle's testimony states he fled "down the hallway to my right" toward the phone. Given that the Forbidden Chamber (where the demon emerged) is to the east of the corridor juncture (Prop 1), and L'Belle was fleeing from the demon, he would have been facing west when the demon emerged from the east. Therefore, "to my right" would be north - which aligns with the phone's location.

But there's a deeper contradiction: L'Belle claims he was in the hallway hiding when he heard the confession (Testimony 0), then fled toward the phone (Testimony 4). However, Jinxie's Statement (Evidence 12) says the demon fled toward the foyer, which is south of the corridor juncture (Prop 3). This creates a spatial impossibility - both L'Belle and the demon cannot have fled in opposite directions from the same location at the same time.

**Step 4:** If none of the listed propositions apply, explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

The key contradiction is between L'Belle's claim that he fled toward the phone (north) and Jinxie's eyewitness account that the demon fled toward the foyer (south). These are directly opposite directions from the corridor juncture.

No applicable propositions exist for this specific contradiction.

**Step 4:** If none of the listed propositions apply, explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

The contradiction is purely spatial - the evidence shows the victim was lying on the floor (covered by books), while the testimony claims the victim was standing in front of the bookshelves. These two positions are mutually exclusive - one cannot simultaneously be lying on the floor and standing upright.

- Testimony 4: "I was in the yard, but the door was wide open, so I could keep watch on the dressing room"

- Prop 11: "If Geiru is in the yard and Simon is in the hallway, then Geiru is in a different room from Simon"
- Prop 15: "If Geiru is in the yard and the yard door is open and the dressing room door is behind the yard door, then Geiru cannot see the dressing room"

**Step 4:** If none of the listed propositions apply, explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

The contradiction is clear: Geiru cannot simultaneously be in the hallway in front of the dressing room (Testimony 1) and in the yard (Testimony 4) during the same time period (4 PM to 4:20 PM). Additionally, the spatial layout described in Evidence 1 makes it impossible for Geiru to "keep watch on the dressing room" from the yard when the yard door is open, as it blocks the view.

**Step 2:** **Check if any spatial propositions** from the list above apply to this contradiction

**Step 3:** **If applicable propositions exist,** explicitly state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

**Step 4:** **If none of the listed propositions apply,** explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

**Step 5:** **Provide your answer in JSON format**

## CRITICAL OUTPUT REQUIREMENT:
**Your answer must end with a JSON format like so:** {"evidence": 2, "testimony": 3}

**The answer JSON should strictly be the last line, with nothing before or after it.**

LLM OUTPUT (RAW RESPONSE):
------------------------------
**Step 1:** Analyze the spatial contradiction between evidences and testimonies

Looking at the testimonies, Nash claims in Testimony 3 that "The bloke wot owned the place was 'oldin' a gun, so 'e should 'ave just fired instead of yellin' at the girl!" This suggests Windibank was holding a gun and yelling at Gina.

However, the Autopsy Report (Evidence 9) states Windibank died from "a single bullet wound from the back" with "no other signs of trauma." This indicates Windibank was shot from behind, which would make it impossible for him to be facing Gina while holding a gun and yelling at her.

**Step 2:** Check if any spatial propositions from the list above apply to this contradiction

Prop 1: "If Windibank is shot from behind then the bullet comes from behind Windibank" - This applies directly to the contradiction. If Windibank was shot from behind, the bullet came from behind him, making it impossible for him to be facing Gina while holding a gun.

**Step 3:** If applicable propositions exist, explicitly state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

Prop 1 reveals the contradiction: If Windibank was shot from behind (as stated in the Autopsy Report), then the bullet came from behind him. This directly contradicts Nash's testimony that Windibank was facing Gina while holding a gun and yelling at her, as someone shot from behind would not be in a position to face and yell at someone in front of them.

**Step 4:** Not applicable - we found applicable propositions.

**Step 5:** Provide your answer in JSON format

{"evidence": 9, "testimony": 3}

More importantly, if Fawles were to shove someone from behind, that person would need to be positioned with their back to the broken east end, but the photo shows Valerie in that position, not Dahlia.

**Step 4:** The spatial reasoning reveals that the testimony about being shoved from behind contradicts the photographic evidence showing Valerie (the victim) at the bridge edge, not Dahlia.

From Evidence 4 (Crime Photo): The victim appears dead in the trunk, wearing a coat but no scarf. The coat is dry and clean.

From Evidence 5 (Scarf): The scarf was found on Dusky Bridge, wet and muddy.

The contradiction lies in Testimony 4 claiming "the scarf fell off" during the stabbing, but the spatial evidence shows the scarf was found on the bridge (wet and muddy) while the victim was found in the trunk without the scarf and with a dry coat.

**Step 2:** Check if any spatial propositions apply to this contradiction

Looking at the propositions, we need to examine how the scarf could have fallen off during the stabbing and ended up on the bridge while Valerie ended up in the trunk.

**Step 3:** Apply relevant spatial propositions

Prop 1: If Valerie is hit from behind then Valerie falls forward
Prop 2: If Valerie falls forward and the scarf is on Valerie then the scarf falls forward with Valerie

According to these propositions, if Valerie was hit from behind (as stated in Testimony 3), she would fall forward, and if she was wearing the scarf (as shown in Witness's Photo), the scarf would fall forward with her. However, the scarf was found on the bridge (Evidence 5), not with Valerie in the trunk (Evidence 4), which contradicts the expected spatial relationship.

**Step 4:** The spatial propositions reveal that if Valerie was wearing the scarf when hit from behind, the scarf should have fallen forward with her, not been left behind on the bridge. This creates a contradiction between Testimony 4 (claiming the scarf fell off during the stabbing) and the spatial evidence showing the scarf was found separately on the bridge.

------------------------------
**Step 1:** Analyze the spatial contradiction between evidences and testimonies

Looking at the evidence and testimonies, we need to find a spatial contradiction. The key evidence is Crime Photo 1 (Evidence 2) which shows Smith "slumped in a chair and leaning back." This indicates Smith fell backward. 

From Olga's testimonies, particularly Testimony 3 states "The one who was winning... dah, it was victim!" This establishes that Smith was winning the poker game. Testimony 5 states "The moment loss was decided, defendant grabs bottle from table and..." implying Wright grabbed the bottle and struck Smith.

**Step 2:** Check if any spatial propositions apply to this contradiction

The spatial propositions that apply here are:
- Prop 3: If Smith was hit from the front then Smith falls backward
- Prop 4: If Smith was hit from behind then Smith falls forward

**Step 3:** Apply the propositions to reveal the contradiction

Since Crime Photo 1 shows Smith leaning backward (falling backward), according to Prop 3, this means Smith was hit from the front. However, if Smith was winning (Testimony 3) and Wright grabbed the bottle from the table (Testimony 5), this would place Wright in front of Smith when striking. This creates a contradiction because:

- If Wright was in front of Smith (as would be natural during a poker game across a table), hitting from the front would make Smith fall backward (consistent with photo)
- But the spatial arrangement suggests Wright would have to reach across the table to strike, which creates an implausible scenario given the bottle's position and the natural seating arrangement

The contradiction lies between the spatial implication of Smith falling backward (hit from front) and the testimony that Smith was winning, which would make it difficult for Wright to strike from the front in a natural poker game setup.

**Step 4:** The spatial propositions do apply - Prop 3 specifically explains that falling backward indicates being hit from the front, which aligns with the evidence but contradicts the implied spatial arrangement from the testimonies.

**Step 5:** {"evidence": 2, "testimony": 3}

- Testimony 0: After stabbing, Terry picked up Valerie in his arms
- Testimony 1: He carried her over to the car

- Prop 7: If the bridge is 40 feet above Eagle River then Eagle River is out of reach from the bridge
- Prop 8: If the sharp rock bed is under the bridge then the rock bed is out of reach from the bridge
- Prop 11: If the coat in the trunk is dry and clean then the coat did not pass through the wet and muddy area under the bridge
- Prop 13: If the victim was carried from the bridge to the car then the victim's body passed through the space between the bridge and the car

This directly contradicts Testimony 3, which claims Terry carried the body from the bridge to the car and hid it in the trunk, as this would require passing through the muddy area below.

**Step 4:** The contradiction is clearly established through the spatial propositions
